I work as a cashier, and lately at the register, I just... I keep getting stuck doing things that don't really fit the moment. \n\nLike, I'll start tapping the barcode scanner in a certain pattern, or I'll just completely freeze with my hand hovering over the open cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting for their change. It's been happening a lot, and... well, last week it actually got me written up by my manager. So, I figured I needed to come in and see someone about it.\n\nDoctor: During these moments when you freeze or tap in a pattern, are you fully aware of what's happening around you and able to hear the customer, or does it feel like you 'blank out' and lose track of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I don't think I completely blank out, because I know I'm still standing there at work. But... I guess I'm not fully focused on the customer either. \n\nIt's more like I just get stuck in the movement. I might hear them sighing or asking a question, but they just sort of fade into the background. I just can't seem to snap out of it and hand them their change right away. I haven't really noticed if I lose track of time, just that I can't stop what I'm doing to move on to the next step.\n\nDoctor: Before the tapping or freezing starts, do you notice any urge, tension, or anxious feeling that makes you feel like you need to do it, or does it just happen without any warning feeling beforehand?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel an urge or anything like that. There's no buildup or anxious feeling before it happens. It's just... one second I'm scanning items or getting change, and the next second I'm just doing the tapping or I'm frozen. It just sort of happens on its own, without any warning.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of the tapping or freezing, if someone touched your arm or called your name loudly, would you be able to stop and respond to them, or does it feel like you have no control over stopping until it runs its course?\n\nPatient: I think... if someone actually touched my arm or yelled my name, it might pull me out of it. But usually, the customers just stand there waiting quietly, or maybe just sighing. \n\nSo, like I was saying earlier, I just stay stuck doing things that don't fit the situation at all. I'll just keep tapping the scanner in that pattern, or my hand just stays frozen over the open cash drawer. I really don't feel like I have any control over stopping it myself once it starts. It just kind of has to run its course. And since I can't just stop it on my own, that's exactly why it became such an issue and got me written up last week.\n\nDoctor: Has this tapping or freezing been happening since childhood, or is this something that only started recently, say within the last few months?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been happening since I was a kid. It's definitely more recent. I'd say the freezing and the tapping... and, well, the voices I mentioned earlier... it's all been going on for about the last three months or so.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned hearing voices a moment ago—can you tell me more about that? What do the voices say, and do they sound like they're coming from inside your own mind or from outside, as if someone else were speaking?\n\nPatient: They definitely sound like they're coming from outside of me, like... real people talking. Most days I hear what sounds like a couple of people talking just off to the side of me. Sometimes they'll call my name, sounding like they're back in the stockroom. Or... or they'll say I rang something up wrong while I'm at the register. But whenever I turn around to look, there's nobody there. And I know no one else hears it, because the customers never react. It's just me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent medical illnesses, head injuries, new medications, or substance use, or noticed any other physical symptoms like fever, unusual movements, or confusion around the time these episodes started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't been sick, and I definitely haven't hit my head or anything. I'm not taking any new medications... and I don't use drugs or drink heavily. \n\nAs for the physical stuff, I haven't had a fever or felt physically ill. I mean, physically, I feel completely fine. It just all started happening without any obvious reason like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I might hear them sighing or asking a question, but they just sort of fade into the background. I just can't seem to snap out of it and hand them their change right away. I haven't really noticed if I lose track of time, just that I can't stop what I'm doing to move on to the next step.\n\nDoctor: Before the tapping or freezing starts, do you notice any urge, tension, or anxious feeling that makes you feel like you need to do it, or does it just happen without any warning feeling beforehand?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel an urge or anything like that. There's no buildup or anxious feeling before it happens. It's just... one second I'm scanning items or getting change, and the next second I'm just doing the tapping or I'm frozen. It just sort of happens on its own, without any warning.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of the tapping or freezing, if someone touched your arm or called your name loudly, would you be able to stop and respond to them, or does it feel like you have no control over stopping until it runs its course?\n\nPatient: I think... if someone actually touched my arm or yelled my name, it might pull me out of it. But usually, the customers just stand there waiting quietly, or maybe just sighing. \n\nSo, like I was saying earlier, I just stay stuck doing things that don't fit the situation at all. I'll just keep tapping the scanner in that pattern, or my hand just stays frozen over the open cash drawer. I really don't feel like I have any control over stopping it myself once it starts. It just kind of has to run its course. And since I can't just stop it on my own, that's exactly why it became such an issue and got me written up last week.\n\nDoctor: Has this tapping or freezing been happening since childhood, or is this something that only started recently, say within the last few months?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been happening since I was a kid. It's definitely more recent. I'd say the freezing and the tapping... and, well, the voices I mentioned earlier... it's all been going on for about the last three months or so.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned hearing voices a moment ago—can you tell me more about that? What do the voices say, and do they sound like they're coming from inside your own mind or from outside, as if someone else were speaking?\n\nPatient: They definitely sound like they're coming from outside of me, like... real people talking. Most days I hear what sounds like a couple of people talking just off to the side of me. Sometimes they'll call my name, sounding like they're back in the stockroom. Or... or they'll say I rang something up wrong while I'm at the register. But whenever I turn around to look, there's nobody there. And I know no one else hears it, because the customers never react. It's just me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent medical illnesses, head injuries, new medications, or substance use, or noticed any other physical symptoms like fever, unusual movements, or confusion around the time these episodes started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't been sick, and I definitely haven't hit my head or anything. I'm not taking any new medications... and I don't use drugs or drink heavily. \n\nAs for the physical stuff, I haven't had a fever or felt physically ill. I mean, physically, I feel completely fine. It just all started happening without any obvious reason like that.\n\nDoctor: Besides the freezing and tapping, have you noticed other things like your body staying in an unusual position for a long time without you moving it, resisting when someone tries to move your arm or leg, going completely mute, or repeating words or movements that others do around you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't get stuck in weird positions... and I definitely don't stop talking completely or repeat what other people are saying or doing. It's really just the freezing up for a minute and the tapping that I told you about. I mean, nobody has tried to physically move my arms or legs or anything while I'm at work, so I don't know about the resisting part, but... yeah, no, none of those other things you mentioned.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I work as a cashier, and lately at the register, I just... I keep getting stuck doing things that don't really fit the moment. \n\nLike, I'll start tapping the barcode scanner in a certain pattern, or I'll just completely freeze with my hand hovering over the open cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting for their change. It's been happening a lot, and... well, last week it actually got me written up by my manager. So, I figured I needed to come in and see someone about it.\n\nDoctor: During these moments when you freeze or tap in a pattern, are you fully aware of what's happening around you and able to hear the customer, or does it feel like you 'blank out' and lose track of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I don't think I completely blank out, because I know I'm still standing there at work. But... I guess I'm not fully focused on the customer either. \n\nIt's more like I just get stuck in the movement. I might hear them sighing or asking a question, but they just sort of fade into the background. I just can't seem to snap out of it and hand them their change right away. I haven't really noticed if I lose track of time, just that I can't stop what I'm doing to move on to the next step.\n\nDoctor: Before the tapping or freezing starts, do you notice any urge, tension, or anxious feeling that makes you feel like you need to do it, or does it just happen without any warning feeling beforehand?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel an urge or anything like that. There's no buildup or anxious feeling before it happens. It's just... one second I'm scanning items or getting change, and the next second I'm just doing the tapping or I'm frozen. It just sort of happens on its own, without any warning.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of the tapping or freezing, if someone touched your arm or called your name loudly, would you be able to stop and respond to them, or does it feel like you have no control over stopping until it runs its course?\n\nPatient: I think... if someone actually touched my arm or yelled my name, it might pull me out of it. But usually, the customers just stand there waiting quietly, or maybe just sighing. \n\nSo, like I was saying earlier, I just stay stuck doing things that don't fit the situation at all. I'll just keep tapping the scanner in that pattern, or my hand just stays frozen over the open cash drawer. I really don't feel like I have any control over stopping it myself once it starts. It just kind of has to run its course. What do the voices say, and do they sound like they're coming from inside your own mind or from outside, as if someone else were speaking?\n\nPatient: They definitely sound like they're coming from outside of me, like... real people talking. It's just me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent medical illnesses, head injuries, new medications, or substance use, or noticed any other physical symptoms like fever, unusual movements, or confusion around the time these episodes started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. So, I figured I needed to come in and see someone about it.\n\nDoctor: During these moments when you freeze or tap in a pattern, are you fully aware of what's happening around you and able to hear the customer, or does it feel like you 'blank out' and lose track of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I don't think I completely blank out, because I know I'm still standing there at work. But... I might hear them sighing or asking a question, but they just sort of fade into the background. I just can't seem to snap out of it and hand them their change right away. I haven't really noticed if I lose track of time, just that I can't stop what I'm doing to move on to the next step.\n\nDoctor: Before the tapping or freezing starts, do you notice any urge, tension, or anxious feeling that makes you feel like you need to do it, or does it just happen without any warning feeling beforehand?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel an urge or anything like that. It's just me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent medical illnesses, head injuries, new medications, or substance use, or noticed any other physical symptoms like fever, unusual movements, or confusion around the time these episodes started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. So, I figured I needed to come in and see someone about it.\n\nDoctor: During these moments when you freeze or tap in a pattern, are you fully aware of what's happening around you and able to hear the customer, or does it feel like you 'blank out' and lose track of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I don't think I completely blank out, because I know I'm still standing there at work. But... I guess I'm not fully focused on the customer either. \n\nIt's more like I just get stuck in the movement. I might hear them sighing or asking a question, but they just sort of fade into the background. I just can't seem to snap out of it and hand them their change right away. I haven't really noticed if I lose track of time, just that I can't stop what I'm doing to move on to the next step.\n\nDoctor: Before the tapping or freezing starts, do you notice any urge, tension, or anxious feeling that makes you feel like you need to do it, or does it just happen without any warning feeling beforehand?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel an urge or anything like that. There's no buildup or anxious feeling before it happens. It's just... one second I'm scanning items or getting change, and the next second I'm just doing the tapping or I'm frozen. It just sort of happens on its own, without any warning.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the middle of the tapping or freezing, if someone touched your arm or called your name loudly, would you be able to stop and respond to them, or does it feel like you have no control over stopping until it runs its course?\n\nPatient: I think... if someone actually touched my arm or yelled my name, it might pull me out of it. But usually, the customers just stand there waiting quietly, or maybe just sighing. \n\nSo, like I was saying earlier, I just stay stuck doing things that don't fit the situation at all. I'll just keep tapping the scanner in that pattern, or my hand just stays frozen over the open cash drawer. I really don't feel like I have any control over stopping it myself once it starts. It just kind of has to run its course. And since I can't just stop it on my own, that's exactly why it became such an issue and got me written up last week.\n\nDoctor: Has this tapping or freezing been happening since childhood, or is this something that only started recently, say within the last few months?\n\nPatient: No, it hasn't been happening since I was a kid. It's definitely more recent. I'd say the freezing and the tapping... and, well, the voices I mentioned earlier... it's all been going on for about the last three months or so.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned hearing voices a moment ago—can you tell me more about that? What do the voices say, and do they sound like they're coming from inside your own mind or from outside, as if someone else were speaking?\n\nPatient: They definitely sound like they're coming from outside of me, like... real people talking. Most days I hear what sounds like a couple of people talking just off to the side of me. Sometimes they'll call my name, sounding like they're back in the stockroom. Or... or they'll say I rang something up wrong while I'm at the register. But whenever I turn around to look, there's nobody there. And I know no one else hears it, because the customers never react. It's just me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent medical illnesses, head injuries, new medications, or substance use, or noticed any other physical symptoms like fever, unusual movements, or confusion around the time these episodes started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't been sick, and I definitely haven't hit my head or anything. I'm not taking any new medications... and I don't use drugs or drink heavily. \n\nAs for the physical stuff, I haven't had a fever or felt physically ill. I mean, physically, I feel completely fine. It just all started happening without any obvious reason like that.\n\nDoctor: Besides the freezing and tapping, have you noticed other things like your body staying in an unusual position for a long time without you moving it, resisting when someone tries to move your arm or leg, going completely mute, or repeating words or movements that others do around you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't get stuck in weird positions... and I definitely don't stop talking completely or repeat what other people are saying or doing. It's really just the freezing up for a minute and the tapping that I told you about. I mean, nobody has tried to physically move my arms or legs or anything while I'm at work, so I don't know about the resisting part, but... yeah, no, none of those other things you mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Have you or anyone else noticed you staring blankly into space for long periods, making unusual facial grimaces, or seeming completely unresponsive to your surroundings during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't think I stare into space or make weird faces. And I'm pretty sure I'm still aware of what's going on around me... even when I freeze up, I can still hear the voices and see the customers. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm completely unresponsive.\n\nDoctor: How many times a day would you say the freezing or tapping episodes happen, and do they occur every single day, or only some days?\n\nPatient: Well, it happens a lot. I'd say multiple times a day, every day. That's actually why I came in... at the register, I just keep getting stuck doing things that don't fit the moment. Like, I'll be ringing someone up and I'll start tapping the scanner in a specific pattern, or I'll just freeze completely with my hand hovering right over the cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting. It happens so often that last week it actually got me written up at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed times when you become excessively restless or agitated without any clear purpose, or when you resist and push back if someone tries to guide or move your body?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any of that. I don't really get restless or agitated for no reason. I mostly just... well, I just get stuck. And like I mentioned a little bit ago, nobody has actually tried to physically guide me or move my body while I'm at the store, so I haven't pushed back or resisted anyone. So, no, none of that.\n\nDoctor: Do the freezing and tapping episodes happen even when you're not hearing the voices at that moment, or do they only occur right when the voices are speaking to you?\n\nPatient: Well, they happen together.
